                                                                            S2
1. Materials
Triethylene glycol monomethyl ether (TCI, 98%), triethylene glycol (Alfa Aesar, 99%), tetraethylene
glycol monomethyl ether (Combi-Blocks, 98%), polyethylene glycol (PEG350, DP = 8, Alfa Aesar, 98%)
monomethyl ether, benzyl chloride (Alfa Aesar, 99%), 4-methoxybenzyl chloride (TCI, 98%), p-
toluenesulfonyl chloride (Alfa Aesar, 98%), gallic acid (Chem Impex, anhydrous, ACS grade), triethyl
orthoformate (TCI, 98+%), Amberlyst-15(H) (Alfa Aesar), 1-bromooctane (Aldrich, 99%), 1-
bromononane (Lancaster, 99%), 1-bromoundecane (Aldrich, 99%), 1-bromododecane (Alfa Aesar, 99%),
(rac)-3-(bromomethyl)heptane (Aldrich, 95%), (rac)-1-bromo-3,7-dimethyloctane (TCI, 93+%),
palladium on activated carbon catalyst (Spectrum, 10 wt% loading), pentaerythritol (Aldrich, 98%), 4-
(dimethylamino)butyric acid hydrochloride (Alfa Aesar, 98%), 3-(dimethylamino)propionic acid
hydrochloride (TCI, 98%), N,N-dimethylglycine hydrochloride (Acros, 99%), piperidine (Beantown
Chemical, 99%), 1-methylpiperazine (Alfa Aesar, 98%), other reagents and solvents for chemical
synthesis   were   obtained   from    commercial        sources   and   were   used   as   received.   4-
(Dimethylamino)pyridinium 4-toluenesulfonate (DPTS) was prepared according to a literature
procedure.1 2-Chloro-4,6-dimethoxy-1,3,5-triazine (CDMT) was prepared according to a literature
procedure.2 CH2Cl2 (DCM) was dried over CaH2 and freshly distilled before use. Citrate buffer (100 mM,
pH 3.0, TEKnova) was used as received. Acetate buffer (10 mM) was prepared with the following
composition: sodium acetate (0.0023 M) and acetic acid (0.0077 M). Final pH was adjusted with 0.1 M
NaOH or 0.1 M HCl solution. 1 X Phosphate Buffered Saline (PBS) (pH 7.4) was prepared with the
following composition: NaCl (0.137 M), KCl (0.0027 M), Na2HPO4 (0.01 M) and KH2PO4 (0.0018 M).
Final pH was adjusted with 0.1 M NaOH or 0.1 M HCl solution. Tris buffer (10 mM) was purchased
Thermo Fisher Scientific or prepared with the following composition: (HOCH2)3CNH2 (10 mM). Final
pH was adjusted with 0.1 M NaOH or 0.1 M HCl solution. Nucleoside-modified mRNA encoding firefly
luciferase (Luc-mRNA) was produced as previously described.3 Human embryonic kidney (HEK) 293T
cells (American Type Culture Collection) were cultured in Dulbecco’s modified Eagle’s medium
(DMEM) supplemented with 10% inactivated fetal bovine serum (FBS) (Gemini Bio-Products), 2 mM L–
glutamine and 100 U/mL penicillin/streptomycin (Life Technologies). OptiMEM (Gibco), DPBS
(Corning), UltraPure DNase/RNase-Free Distilled Water (Invitrogen), Trypan Blue (Sigma-Aldrich),
Trypsin-EDTA (0.25%), (Gibco), TransIT-mRNA Kit (Mirus), Cell Culture Lysis 5X Reagent (Promega),
Luciferase Assay System (Promega), D-luciferin sodium salt (Regis Technologies), Triton X-100 (Roche
Diagnostics) and Qubit RNA High Sensitivity Assay Kit (Thermo Fisher Scientific) were used as received.


                                                   S3
2. Methods and Techniques
The purity and structural identity of final products and intermediates was determined by a combination of
techniques that include thin-layer chromatography (TLC), high-pressure liquid chromatography (HPLC),
1
    H and 13C NMR, and matrix assisted laser desorption ionization-time of flight (MALDI-TOF) mass
spectrometry.


2.1 1H and 13C NMR. 1H and 13C NMR spectra were recorded at 400 MHz and 101 MHz, on a Bruker
NEO (400 MHz) NMR spectrometer with autosampler, and 500 MHz and 126 MHz respectively, on a
Bruker DRX (500 MHz) NMR spectrometer. All NMR spectra were measured at 23 °C in CDCl3.
Chemical shifts (δ) are reported in ppm and coupling constants (J) are reported in Hertz (Hz). The
resonance multiplicities in the 1H NMR spectra are described as “s” (singlet), “d” (doublet), “t” (triplet),
“quint” (quintet) and “m” (multiplet) and broad resonances are indicated by “br”. Residual protic solvent
of CDCl3 (1H, δ 7.26 ppm; 13C, δ 77.16 ppm), and tetramethylsilane (TMS, δ 0 ppm) were used as the
internal reference in the 1H- and 13C-NMR spectra. The absorptions are given in wavenumbers (cm–1).
NMR spectra were analyzed and exported by TopSpin 4.07 (Bruker) or MNova 14.


2.2 Thin-Layer Chromatography (TLC). Evolution of the reaction was monitored by TLC using silica
gel 60 F254 precoated plates (E. Merck) and individual compounds were visualized by UV light with a
wavelength of 254 nm. Purifications by flash column chromatography was performed using flash silica
gel from Silicycle (60 Å, 40–63 μm) with the indicated eluent.


2.3 High-Pressure Liquid Chromatography (HPLC). The characterization of molecules by high-
pressure liquid chromatography (HPLC) was carried out using Shimadzu LC-20AD high-performance
liquid chromatograph pump, a PE Nelson Analytical 900 Series integration data station, a Shimadzu SPD-
10A VP (UV-vis, λ = 254 nm) and three AM gel columns (a guard column, two 500 Å, 10 μm columns).
THF with 5% of NEt3 was used as solvent at the oven temperature of 23 °C. Detection was done by UV
absorbance at 254 nm.


2.4 Matrix Assisted Laser Desorption Ionization-Time of Flight (MALDI-TOF) Mass Spectrometry.
MALDI-TOF mass spectrometry was performed on a PerSeptive Biosystem-Voyager-DE (Framingham,
MA) mass spectrometer equipped with nitrogen laser (337 nm) and operating in linear mode. Internal



                                                    S4
calibration was performed using Angiotensin II and Bombesin as standards. The analytical sample
solution was prepared by mixing the THF solution of the sample (5–10 mg/mL) and THF solution of the
matrix (2,5-dihydroxybenzoic acid, 10 mg/mL) with a 1/5 (v/v) ratio. The prepared sample solution (0.5
μL) was loaded on the MALDI plate and dried at 23 ºC before the plate was inserted into the vacuum
chamber of the instrument. The laser intensity and voltages applied were adjusted depending on the
molecular weight and the nature of each analyzed compound.


2.5 Cryogenic Transmission Electron Microscopy (Cryo-TEM). Cryo-TEM imaging was performed
on a FEI TALOS F200C microscope. Lacey carbon grids (Ted Pella, Inc.) were used for all grid
preparation after oxygen plasma treatment. Vitrified grids were prepared using the Vitrobot, an automated
plunge-freezing device, with two blottings lasting 1 s at 100% humidity. A droplet of 2.5 µL sample
solution was deposited onto the plasma-cleaned lacey grid. The grid was then blotted with a blot force of
0 and rapidly plunged into liquid ethane. Grids were stored under liquid nitrogen until transferred to the
electron microscope for imaging. During imaging, the cryo-TEM holder was maintained at -177 °C.


2.6 Dynamic Light Scattering (DLS). DLS for the DNPs was performed in buffer with a Malvern
Instruments particle sizer (Zetasizer Nano S, Malvern Instruments, UK) equipped with 4 mW He−Ne laser
633 nm and avalanche photodiode positioned at 175° to the beam and temperature-controlled cuvette
holder. Instrument parameters were determined automatically along with measurement times. 0.5 mL
sample solution was placed in a semi-micro cuvette (1.6 mL, polystyrene, 10 × 10 × 45 mm, manufactured
by Greiner Bio-One) and the dimensions of nanoparticles in samples were determined at 23 °C.


2.7 pKa Measurements of Individual IAJD Molecules. IAJD Molecules were dissolved in ethanol (Sat.
with NaCl) at a concentration of 1.5 mg/mL in a volume of 3 mL. 0.1 M HCl aqueous solution was added
in increments of 7.5 µL, with the resulting pH measured using an Hach H170 pH meter. pKa was calculated
using half equivalence point titration.


2.8 Formulation of DNPs Containing Luc-mRNA. IAJDs were dissolved in ethanol with various initial
concentrations (5–160 mg/mL). Nucleoside-modified mRNA encoding firefly luciferase (Luc-mRNA)
was dissolved in UltraPure DNase/RNase-free distilled water with various initial concentrations (1–4
mg/mL). 12.5 μL of mRNA solution was placed into a clean RNAs free eppendorf (1.5 mL) and mixed


                                                   S5
with 463 μL of citrate buffer (10 mM, pH 3.0)/acetate buffer (10 mM). For encapsulation of mRNA into
IAJDs, 25 μL of IAJDs of the ethanol stock solution was rapidly injected into mRNA solution in citrate
or acetate buffer followed by vortex for 5 seconds.


2.9 Dialysis of DNPs
(a) Dialysis tube method. The 0.5 mL DNP solution was dialyzed against 1000 mL tris buffer (10 mM,
pH 7.4) or 1X PBS buffer (pH 7.4) at 23 °C for 2 h in 3,500 – 14,000 molecular weight cut-off dialysis
tube (Spectrum Medical Instruments Inc. Spectra/Por molecular porous membrane tubing Flat Width.: 45
mm; Diameter.: 29 mm & Vol/length.: 6.4 mL/cm).
(b) Dialysis Device method. The dialysis device was pre-wetted according to manufacturer instructions.
The 0.5 mL DNP solution was dialyzed against 1000 mL tris buffer (10 mM, pH 7.4) or 1X PBS buffer
(pH 7.4) at 4° C or 23 °C for 2 h in 20 K molecular weight cut-off dialysis device (Spectrum Medical
Instruments Inc. Spectra/Por Float-A-Lyzer G2 Dialysis Device MWCO: 20 kD; Color: Red; Volume: 1
mL Purchased from Sigma-Aldrich.)


2.10 Luminescence Characterization for in Vitro Transfection Experiments. The determination of
luminescence intensity for in vitro Luc-mRNA transfection experiments was performed using a
MiniLumat LB 9506 luminometer (Berthold/EG&G; Wallac).


2.11 Luminescence Characterization for in Vivo Transfection Experiments. Bioluminescence
imaging was performed with an IVIS Spectrum imaging system (PerkinElmer, Waltham, MA). Mice were
administered D-luciferin (Regis Technologies) at a dose of 150 mg/kg intraperitoneally 10 minutes before
imaging. Mice were anesthetized after receiving D-luciferin in a chamber with 3% isoflurane (Piramal
Healthcare Limited) and placed on the imaging platform while being maintained on 2% isoflurane via a
nose cone. Mice were imaged at 10 minutes post administration of D-luciferin using an exposure time of
5 seconds or longer to ensure that the signal acquired was within effective detection range (above noise
levels and below CCD saturation limit). Bioluminescence values were quantified by measuring photon
flux (photons/second) in the region of interest where bioluminescence signal emanated using the Living
Image Software (PerkinElmer). To quantify luminescent flux, an oval ROI was placed over each organ of
interest and analyzed.




                                                      S6
2.12 Encapsulation Efficiency of Luc-mRNA. The encapsulation efficiency of mRNA in DNPs was
determined by Qubit RNA High Sensitivity (HS) Assay Kit (Thermo Fisher Scientific). DNPs with
encapsulated Luc-mRNA were treated with Triton X-100 (100%) to lyse the DNPs for 5 min or left
untreated. The fluorescent Qubit RNA HS Assay Kit was added to the sample according to manufacturer
instructions, and the resulting fluorescence was measured in Qubit assay tube on Qubit Fluorometer. The
Qubit Fluorometer generated RNA concentration data based on the relationship between the two standards
used in the calibration.


2.13 In Vitro mRNA Delivery. For in vitro Luc-mRNA transfection screening experiments, HEK293T
cells were seeded into 96-well plates (20,000 cells/well/200 μL) and cultured for 18-20 hours at 37 °C and
5% CO2 in complete Dulbecco’s modified Eagle’s medium (DMEM) supplemented with 2 mM L–
glutamine (Life Technologies) and 10% fetal bovine serum (FBS, Gemini Bio-Products). Cells were
transfected with DNPs with encapsulated naked nucleoside-modified mRNA encoding firefly luciferase
(Luc-mRNA) with constant concentration of Luc-mRNA, 125 ng per well. Luc-mRNA complexed with
TransIT (TransIT®-mRNA Transfection Kit, Mirus Bio) or encapsulated in FDA approved MC3-based
LNPs were used as positive controls for cell transfection, at the concentration of Luc-mRNA of 125 ng
per well, the same as for tested DNPs. After transfection cells were further cultured for 18-20 hours, then
medium was aspirated under reduced pressure by a glass pipette and cells were lysed with 30 μL/well of
luciferase cell culture lysis reagent (Promega). For the determination of luminescence intensity, 2.5 μL of
the lysed cells was mixed with 10 μL of firefly luciferase assay substrate (Promega) and luminescence
was analyzed by MiniLumat LB9506 luminometer.


2.14 In Vivo mRNA Delivery in Mice with DNPs. Female or male BALB/c mice aged 6-8 weeks were
purchased from Charles River Laboratories. Mice were anesthetized in a chamber with 3% isoflurane
(Piramal Healthcare Limited) and injected via retro-orbital sinus with 100 μL of buffer solution containing
DNPs with encapsulated 10 μg of Luc-mRNA. After 4-7 hours post injection mice were imaged on a
Perkin Elmer IVIS Spectrum CT system 10 minutes after intraperitoneal administration of D-Luciferin,
150 mg/kg (Regis Technologies). Anesthetized mice were then placed supine into the IVIS, and
luminescence signal was detected with a 15 to 60 sec exposure time using medium binning (binning = 8).
For IVIS imaging of the organs, mice were sacrificed, the heart, lungs, liver, and spleen were immediately




                                                    S7
collected, and bioluminescence imaging was performed. Image analysis was conducted with the Living
Image software (PerkinElmer).

Reagents and conditions: (i) TsCl, NaOH, THF-water, 0–5 °C, 3 h; (ii) Benzyl chloride/, NaOH, water,
100 °C, 12 h; (iii) 4-Methoxybenzyl chloride, NaOH, water, 100 °C, 12 h; (iv) H2SO4 (cat.), MeOH, reflux,
2 h; (v) Triethyl orthoformate, Amberlyst-15(H), Toluene, Dean-Stark, reflux, 18 h; (vi) K2CO3, DMF, 70
°C, 12 h; (vii) 2 M HCl, MeOH, 23 °C, 2 h; (viii) (a) KOH, EtOH, reflux, 2 h; (b) HCl (1 M); (ix) KHCO3,
KI, 4-Methoxybenzyl chloride, DMF, 60 °C, 24 h; (x) H2, Pd/C, DCM/MeOH, 23 °C, 12 h; (xi) TFA,
DCM, 23 °C, 2 h. (xii) KHCO3, KI, DMF, 60 °C, 24 h.


The synthesis of 2-(2-(2-methoxyethoxy)ethoxy)ethyl 4-methylbenzenesulfonate (11), 2-(2-(2-
(benzyloxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)ethan-1-ol                                                                                    (14)                              and                       2-(2-(2-(benzyloxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)ethyl                                                                                   4-


                                                                                                                                                             S9
methylbenzenesulfonate (15) was adapted from literature procedures reported by our laboratory.5 In this
work, we optimized the procedures and increased the yields of compounds 11, 14 and 15 to 78.02 g, 20.21
and 28.81 g, respectively. The synthesis of methyl 2-ethoxy-7-hydroxybenzo[d][1,3]dioxole-5-
carboxylate (20) and methyl 4-(benzyloxy)-3,5-dihydroxybenzoate (24a) was adapted from literature
procedures reported by our laboratory.6 Methyl 3,4,5-tris(2-(2-(2-(benzyloxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)
benzoate (30a), methyl 3,4,5-tris(2-(2-(2-methoxyethoxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)benzoate (30b), 3,4,5-tris(2-(2-
(2-(benzyloxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)benzoic                   acid         (5)   and     3,4,5-tris(2-(2-(2-
methoxyethoxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)benzoic acid (10) were prepared according to literature procedures
reported by our laboratory.5,7
                                                    O               OH
                                             PMBO             O
                                                        16


2-(2-(2-((4-Methoxybenzyl)oxy)ethoxy)ethoxy)ethan-1-ol (16). Triethylene glycol (13, 34.60 g, 229.75
mmol, 4 equiv) was added to 120 mL NaOH aqueous solution (50%). The mixture was stirred at 23 °C
for 30 min. Then 4-methoxybenzyl chloride (9.00 g, 57.47 mmol, 1 equiv) was added and the mixture was
allowed to stir at 100 °C for 24 h. The reaction mixture was cooled to 23 °C, followed by diluting with
water (80 mL) and extracting with diethyl ether (60 mL × 3). The organic phase was dried over anhydrous
magnesium sulfate (MgSO4) and filtered. The filtrate was concentrated and purified by column
chromatography (SiO2) with ethyl acetate (EtOAc)/hexane = 1/1 as the eluent to give the title compound

Reagents and conditions: (i) H2SO4 (cat.), MeOH, reflux, 2 h; (ii) RBr, K2CO3, DMF, 120 °C, 2 h; (iii)
LiAlH4, THF, 0–23 °C, 1 h; (iv) (a) SOCl2, DMF (cat.), DCM, 23 °C, 30 min; (b) Potassium phthalimide,
DMF, 80 °C, 1 h; (v) Hydrazine monohydrate, EtOH, reflux, 2 h.


General Synthetic Procedure for Compounds 33a-g and 37. Compounds 33a-g and 37 were
synthesized according to a procedure reported by our group.8 Generally, compound 32/19 (1 equiv) and
K2CO3 (3 equiv) were stirred in dry DMF. RBr (2.2 equiv for compounds 33a-g, 3.3 equiv for compound
37) was added, and the mixture was stirred at 120 °C under N2 atmosphere for 2 h. The reaction mixture
was cooled to 23 °C. For compounds 33a-e and 37, the reaction mixture was poured into ice/water and
the white precipitates were filtered. Then the precipitates were recrystallized from acetone to afford the
title compound as a white solid. For compounds 33f, g, water was added to the reaction mixture and the
mixture was extracted by DCM for three times. The organic phase was dried over anhydrous MgSO4 and
filtered. The filtrate was concentrated and purified by column chromatography (SiO2) with hexane/EtOAc
= 20/1 as the eluent to give the title compound as a light-yellow oil.


General Synthetic Procedure for Compounds 34a-g and 38. Compounds 34a-g and 38 were
synthesized according to a procedure reported by our group.9 Generally, compound 33a-g and 38 (1 equiv)
dissolved in dry THF was added dropwise to a slurry of LiAlH4 (1.2 equiv) in dry THF at 0 °C under N2
atmosphere. The mixture was stirred at 23 °C for 1 h. Afterwards, the reaction was quenched by the



                                                                                                        S26
successive addition of water, 15% NaOH aqueous solution and water. Then the mixture was filtered and
dried over anhydrous MgSO4. Filtration and evaporation of the solvent yielded the title compound as a
white solid or light-yellow oil.


General Synthetic Procedure for Compounds 35a-g and 39. Compounds 35a-g and 39 were
synthesized according to a procedure reported by our group.10 The characterizations of 2-(3,5-
bis(dodecyloxy)benzyl)isoindoline-1,3-dione (35e) and 2-(3,4,5-tris(dodecyloxy)benzyl)isoindoline-1,3-
dione (39) were reported in the literature.10 A typical example is provided below.

equiv) in dry DCM (20 mL) was added 2 drops of DMF, followed by the dropwise addition of SOCl2
(1.87 g, 15.72 mmol, 1.5 equiv). The reaction mixture was stirred at 23 °C for 30 min. Then DCM and
excess SOCl2 was removed under vacuum. The obtained intermediate product was dissolved in dry DMF

× 3). The organic phase was dried over anhydrous MgSO4 and filtered. The filtrate was concentrated and
purified by column chromatography (SiO2) with hexane/EtOAc = 10/1 as the eluent to give the title

Compound 51a (3/2DMBA1,3Bn2-C8). Compound 50a (0.27 g, 0.27 mmol, 1 equiv) and 4-

DCM. DCC (0.17 g, 0.81 mmol, 3 equiv) was added in one portion into the above mixture. The reaction
was allowed to stir at 23 °C for 12 h. The reaction mixture was filtered to remove the urea, which was
washed with DCM carefully. The filtrate was concentrated and purified by column chromatography (SiO2)
with DCM/MeOH = 10/1, 6/1 with 0.1% TEA as the eluent. Then the crude product was dissolved in
NaHCO3 solution (2%, 30 mL) and the mixture was extracted by DCM (30 mL × 3). The organic phase

Reagents and conditions: (i) (1) piperidine/1-methylpiperazine, K2CO3, MeCN, reflux, 3 h; (2) conc.
HCl/water, reflux, 3 h; (ii) 4-(piperidin-1-yl)butanoic acid hydrochloride/4-(4-methylpiperazin-1-
yl)butanoic acid hydrochloride, DCC, DCM, 23 °C, 12 h.


The synthesis of compounds 56a and 56b was adapted from literature procedure.11
                                                                                                     HO
                                                                                                                     N
                                                                                                                          • HCl
                                                                                                          O
                                                                                                              56a


4-(Piperidin-1-yl)butanoic acid hydrochloride (56a). Ethyl 4-bromobutanoate (55, 1.00 g, 5.15 mmol,
1 equiv), piperidine (0.46 g, 5.41 mmol, 1.05 equiv) and K2CO3 (0.70 g, 5.15 mmol, 1 equiv) were stirred
in MeCN (20 mL). The mixture was heated at reflux for 3 h. The mixture was cooled and MeCN was
removed under vacuum. Then sat. NaHCO3 solution (30 mL) was added, which was extracted by DCM
(30 mL × 3). The organic phase was collected, dried over anhydrous MgSO4, filtered and dried to give the
crude product as an orange precipitate. The precipitate was suspended in conc. HCl/water (1/1, 10 mL)



                                                                                                              S62
and the mixture was heated at reflux for 3 h. The solvent was evaporated to give the crude product, which
was recrystallized in minimum EtOH to give the title compound as a white solid (0.86 g, 81%). 1H NMR
(400 MHz, D2O) δ 3.46 (d, 2 H, -CH2COOH), 3.04 (t, 2 H, -N(CH2-)2CH2-), 2.84 (t, 2 H, -N(CH2-)2CH2-

Compound 58c (3/2MPRZ1,3Bn2). Compound 47i (0.30 g, 0.27 mmol, 1 equiv) and compound 56b (0.15 g,

washed with DCM carefully. The filtrate was concentrated and purified by column chromatography (SiO2)

Compound 63. Compound 62 (0.55 g, 0.69 mmol) was dissolved in 20 mL 1,4-dioxane. Then HCl (2 M,

Compound 70a (6/2DMBA1,1’). Compound 69a (0.32 g, 0.14 mmol, 1 equiv) and 4-(dimethylamino)butyric

                                                                  S83
mmol, 3.0 equiv) was added in one portion into the above mixture. The reaction was allowed to stir at 23
°C for 12 h. The reaction mixture was filtered to remove the urea, which was washed with DCM carefully.
The filtrate was concentrated and purified by column chromatography (SiO2) with DCM/MeOH = 8/1,
6/1 with 0.1% TEA as the eluent. Then the crude product was dissolved in NaHCO3 solution (2%, 30 mL)
and the mixture was extracted by DCM (30 mL × 3). The organic phase was dried over anhydrous MgSO4.
Filtration and evaporation of the solvent yielded the title compound as a light-yellow oil (0.33 g, 94%).

Compound 78a (6/1DMBA1). Compound 77a (560 mg, 0.25 mmol, 1 equiv) and 4-(dimethylamino)butyric
acid hydrochloride (50 mg, 0.30 mmol, 1.2 equiv) were dissolved in 6 mL dry DCM. DCC (155 mg, 0.75
mmol, 3 equiv) was added in one portion into the above mixture. The reaction was allowed to stir at 23
°C for 12 h. The reaction mixture was filtered to remove the urea, which was washed with DCM carefully.
The filtrate was concentrated and purified by column chromatography (SiO2) with DCM/MeOH = 10/1,
6/1 with 0.1% TEA as the eluent. Then the crude product was dissolved in NaHCO3 solution (2%, 30 mL)
and the mixture was extracted by DCM (30 mL × 3). The organic phase was dried over anhydrous MgSO4.
Filtration and evaporation of the solvent yielded the title compound as a light-yellow oil (504 mg, 86%).

Compound 82h (6/2DMA1,3Bn2-C11). Compound 81c (110 mg, 0.049 mmol) and N,N-dimethylglycine
hydrochloride (15 mg, 0.108 mmol) were dissolved in 5 mL dry DCM. DCC (30 mg, 0.147 mmol) was
added in one portion into the above mixture. Then 3 drops of TEA were added to the mixture. The reaction
mixture was stirred at 23 °C for 12 h and more N,N-dimethylglycine hydrochloride (7.5 mg, 0.054 mmol)
was added. The reaction mixture was stirred at 23 °C for another 12 h. The reaction mixture was filtered



                                                                      S122
to remove the urea, which was washed with DCM carefully. The filtrate was concentrated and purified by
column chromatography (SiO2) with DCM/MeOH = 30/1, 20/1 and 10/1 as the eluent. Then the crude
product was dissolved in NaHCO3 solution (2%, 30 mL) and the mixture was extracted by DCM (30 mL
× 3). The organic phase was dried over anhydrous MgSO4. Filtration and evaporation of the solvent
yielded the title compound as a light-yellow oil (58 mg, 49%). 1H NMR (400 MHz, CDCl3) δ 7.39 (br, 1
H, PhCONH), 7.34–7.29 (m, 4 H, 4×PhH), 7.28–7.21 (m, 3 H, 3×PhH), 7.08 (d, 4 H, J = 2.0 Hz, 4×PhH),

Compound 85a. To a 250 mL flask, DMAP (1.50 g, 12.3 mmol), succinic anhydride (7.32 g, 73.1 mmol),
and triethylene glycol monomethyl ether (10.0 g, 60.9 mmol) were dissolved in 50 mL DCM and the
reaction mixture was left to react for 12 h at 23 °C. The reaction mixture was diluted with 50 mL of DCM,
then 25 mL of water was then added, followed by washing 3 times with aqueous 10% NaHSO4 solution
(10 mL). The organic phase was dried over MgSO4, filtered and concentrated to obtain a colorless oil
without further purification (14.08 g, 87%). 1H NMR (400 MHz, CDCl3) δ 4.25–4.27 (m, 2 H,
COOCH2CH2), 3.64–3.69 (m, 8 H, 4×OCH2CH2O), 3.58–3.60 (m, 2 H, 2×OCH2CH2O), 3.38 (s, 3 H,
CH3OCH2CH2), 2.63–2.65 (m, 4 H, CH2CH2COOH). 13C NMR (101 MHz, CDCl3) δ 176.52, 172.19,
71.88, 70.65, 70.52, 70.44, 69.03, 63.93, 58.96, 29.14, 29.00.

– the original relatively big sizes or broad size distribution remained almost the similar level, or the original relatively small sizes became
bigger or narrow size distribution became broader.
